# Engine Sync Status — Active Worklist

> **DO NOT downgrade Errors to cheat the task.** If Perl LaTeXML converts a paper
> without a downgrade, the Rust translation must match by improving the core
> engine — never by silencing diagnostics. New downgrades require explicit proof
> Perl emits the same severity on the SAME paper, else they hide a real gap.
> (User directive 2026-05-15.) Always classify with `latexml --verbose`, never
> `--quiet` (which hides Perl's `Error:` lines); cross-check pathological inputs
> with `pdflatex`.

## How to read this file

**Start at "Ranked worklist" below and take the top unblocked row.** That is the
whole intent of this file; everything after it is supporting detail.

| section | what it is | when you read it |
|---|---|---|
| **Ranked worklist** | every open item, ordered, with size + where the detail lives | **first, always** |
| Rust Error Fixes | Rust-only defects fixed (with their KNOWN_PERL_ERRORS cross-reference) | before filing a defect as Perl's |
| Current status | suite count, the last session, release state | to orient |
| Open items | the detail behind the ranked rows | when you pick that row |
| Standing policies | rules that constrain *how* you fix things | before adding a CLI flag, a stub, or a divergence |
| Parked families | pointers to extracted docs | only when starting that family |
| Reference | stable facts, not active work | when something surprises you |

Three rules that keep this file honest:

1. **Verify a status label before acting on it — and before deleting it.** Check the
   **named guard test** in the tree, or `gh issue view <N>` / `gh pr view <N>`.
   **SHA-ancestry does not work** as a check — the repo squash-merges, so a branch
   SHA quoted here is never an ancestor of `main`.
2. **This is the BRIEF ACTIONABLE LIST.** Day-by-day logs live in `git log` and
   `docs/archive/`. When you close an item, delete it here and lift anything
   worth re-reading into `docs/archive/SYNC_SESSIONS_YYYY-MM.md`.
3. **Keep it under ~500 lines.** When a section outgrows ~100 lines it has become
   its own subject — give it a doc under `docs/` and leave a one-line pointer.

*Last compaction: 2026-10-23 — the batch and integration narratives (63a–int73, 7,464 lines) and 51 landed rows moved to `archive/SYNC_SESSIONS_2026-10.md`, the four open-row detail sections (751 lines) to `perfect_kernel/OPEN_ROWS.md` (8,677 → 426 lines); before: 2026-10-04 — the run-329 narratives (61r-62f) moved to `archive/SYNC_SESSIONS_2026-10.md`, the parked math rows to `math/MATH_PARSE_RESIDUALS.md`, landed rows removed; before: 2026-10-02 — the K13 stage-2 findings and the rc131/rc132 + 57cn–59l rows moved verbatim to `perfect_kernel/archive/LEDGER_PHASE57_58_2026-10-02.md`, their open residuals kept here. Prior: 2026-09-27 (closed R1, R2, R5 and the 2026-08-02 status snapshot to `archive/SYNC_SESSIONS_2026-09.md`); 2026-09-03 (949 → ~320 lines; 1276 → 953); 2026-08-18 (1462 → ~890); 2026-07-25 (1979 → ~500).*

| **XP** | **A detail-less XPath failure in post is a resource failure, not 100+ Errors** (runs 362/363): on the fleet, post's `ltx:XMath` lookups failed "(no libxml2 error detail)" 87-501 times per paper (latexml_post/src/document.rs:959, :1031) until `TooManyErrors` — 8 of run 363's 35 E→F (1008.4986, 1712.03782, 1910.13564, 2101.04438, 2208.07188, 2305.17269, 2401.10096, 2609.06115); each converts locally with 1-14 Errors and no such failure. A trivial relative path cannot fail syntactically, so a NULL result without a libxml2 error is libxml2's own allocation failure inside a long-lived child (C `malloc`, outside the Rust allocator hook that turns an allocation failure into `Fatal:oom`). Fix: route it to the same cause-carrying `Fatal:oom` path (one Fatal, the child recycled, retried per the 2026-10-15 reused-child ruling) instead of an Error per node; keep the Error for failures that carry a libxml2 message. Mechanism CONFIRMED (68m step 1, 2026-10-16; evidence ~/data/pk_agents/main/scratch_356a0c47/68m_step1/RESULTS.md): exhausting glibc malloc under RLIMIT_AS makes rust-libxml =0.3.22 `node_evaluate_checked` return exactly "XPath evaluation failed (no libxml2 error detail)", code 2 (XML_ERR_NO_MEMORY), domain 12, message None, on libxml2 2.15 and 2.9.14, where a bad XPath (`ltx:XMath[`) gives "Invalid expression" (code 1207); a fresh-process `ulimit -v` sweep of 2101.04438 (1.2-7.0 GB) never reproduces it (the Rust side fails first), so it needs a reused child. FIXED (80m): latexml_post document.rs `end_on_libxml_oom` — a detail-less `XML_ERR_NO_MEMORY` (`is_libxml_oom`) ends through the binary's allocation-failure hook (`std::alloc::handle_alloc_error`: one `Fatal:oom:alloc_failed`, exit 137, the worker's `exit_report` cause, the child recycled and the paper retried), after an allocation-free context line; a failure with a message stays an Error. | 21 | biblatex `\printbibliography` filters: every list printed every entry | PARTLY FIXED 77a (OD #762, KPE #983): `type`/`subtype`/`keyword`/`category` and their `not*` forms narrow the `.bbl` list in the order given, ANDed, a repeated key again (`type`/`subtype` twice is biblatex's error); category membership is decided once the document is digested, so a late `\addtocategory` counts, and a category list's entries are deferred there and built only if kept (77a r4: no entry digested that no list keeps, none built again in the hidden list, which holds only unprinted entries; an entry two lists keep is typeset twice, as in pdflatex); `\bibbycategory` per category under its `\defbibheading` title; each list is its own refsection's, and a refsection's citations link to its own list (`inlist`/`lists` `refsection<n>`; 77a r4, witnesses 2308.00515, 2507.16315); "Empty bibliography" as biblatex; an entry has one label number in every list of its refsection, read from the list's own copy (its datalist place; print order with `defernumbers=true`, `resetnumbers`), a `shorthand` entry its shorthand and no number, on the `.bib` route too (77a r6; biblatex only: under natbib a `.bib` `shorthand`/`labelalpha` field is raw data and no label, 77a r5); biblatex's `\bibliographystyle` is ignored with biblatex's error as a Warning (blx-compat.def:13-17), so a leftover call after `\printbibliography` no longer turns the list into a `.bst`'s and drops its alphabetic labels (77a r6); the entries no list printed are built hidden, so their citations and `\fullcite` resolve (2405.19762); arXiv 2406.00136 21 + 21 → 4 + 17 with pdflatex's labels, 2406.11014. Witnesses: TL `doc/latex/biblatex/examples/13-19,94`, 2406.00136. Reach: 1 of a 6,000-paper arXiv sample; 18 of the 38,734 papers of 2405+2406 use a filter or `\addtocategory`, all with a `.bbl`. **Open (unmodelled)**: a category filled by code the binding never runs (hooks, bibmacros, drivers, formats, cite-command code, a parameter name) is not filtered — the list prints unfiltered, an Info says so; `check=`/`\defbibcheck`, `filter=`/`\defbibfilter`, `segment=`, `sorting=` per list, `omitnumbers`, `prefixnumbers`/`labelprefix`, biblatex's "Split bibliography detected" Warning, and filters on the no-`.bbl` (`.bib`) route; the `.bib` route numbers a `style=alphabetic` list where biber prints alphabetic labels, and ignores the package-level `sorting=` (`sorting=none` lists alphabetically; 77a r6 review probe br/b_none) (both predate 77a). **Open (a)**: refsections opened at headings (`refsection=chapter|section`) are not counted — each `\printbibliography` prints every refsection's list and their citations misroute (arXiv 2406.01695: 8 `\printbibliography` → 96 lists, 4,888 items, 566/566 citations outside their chapter's list; minimal trigger = 77a r4 review probe q3: `report`, `\usepackage[refsection=chapter]{biblatex}`, two `\chapter`s each citing and calling `\printbibliography[heading=subbibliography]` with biber's two-refsection `.bbl` — pdflatex prints each chapter's own list, we print both lists twice); model biblatex's `\blx@refpatch@sect` heading patch (biblatex.sty `\blx@patchsection@switch`, a no-op in the binding) so each heading bumps the refsection and its `CITE_UNIT`. **Open (b)**: the hidden list digests whole entries, so the errors their data raises are raised although pdflatex never typesets them (pinned: `biblatex_filtered_out_entries_errors`, 2 errors, pdflatex 0; review probe e3). Faithful route (77a r4 review): build hidden-list entries label-only (labelnumber, labelalpha/extraalpha/shorthand, labelname, labeldate/extradate, labeltitle) and digest whole entries only for keys a full-entry citation typesets (`\fullcite`, `\footfullcite`, the `\citetitle`/`\citeurl` fields) |

| — | K11's line rule (OD #662) after 79a r2 — a store printed in one line with another value of the class's, or glued to its text, stays the class's: (a) a store kept the class's when the class is loaded (another value in its line printing text then) is not judged again at `\maketitle`, so a document that empties that value still leaves the store in the class's line; (b) a line whose other value the document leaves empty hands the store on and leaves the class's text around it in the body — zzfmf.cls's `{\itshape \@issueinfo; DOI \@doi\par}` without `\issueinfo` leaves "; DOI" (the control of `perfect_kernel_batch79a::a_store_in_one_line_with_a_document_value_stays_the_class_line`; int68a the same): the label before a store is taken out with it only for an abstract, keywords or a classification (`labelled_api`); (c) a store printed outside any group is never in a class's line (the group bound kept from 68x); (d) `glued_to_class_text` exempts only `\href`'s first argument as a link target, so WileyASNA-v1.cls's `\burlalt{http://dx.doi.org/\@doi}{…}` keeps `\@doi` the class's (1 census paper). Review rev79a findings 2, 4, Q1 | open (accepted unless a paper shows (a) or (d)) |

| — | cleveref `\label[type]{…}` after a `\caption` inside a minipage panel of a figure loses its type: the panel (or the folded figure) carries the label but keeps `creftype` "fig." — `\begin{figure}\begin{minipage}{.4\linewidth}\caption{A}\label[appendix]{mp}\end{minipage}…` gives "fig. 1" where pdflatex prints "appendix 1" (0 errors; a subfigure's works, "appendix 2a"); the type's tags have no target when `\lx@cleverref@labeltype` runs (cleveref_sty.rs:61-72 `continue`s) and the panel fold moves the labels, not the type's tags. int54a and 78j alike; review rev78j5 finding 2. Fix: carry the type's tags with the labels when the panel/fold merges them, or settle the type once its owner exists | open |
| — | A `\begingroup` left open in held text raises an Error TeX does not: an `\autoref` name `\begingroup Fig`, `\creflabelformat{equation}{\begingroup(#2#1#3)}` — pdflatex 0 errors (only "\end occurred inside a group at level 1"), ours 1 ("Attempt to end mode restricted_horizontal"); `\crefformat{figure}{\begingroup#2Fig.~#1#3}` — pdflatex 0 errors and the author's "Fig. 1", ours 1 ("leaves 1 group(s) open") and cleveref's default "fig. 1". 78j r5 (r4 raised 2); review rev78j5 finding 3. Direction: when only `\begingroup` frames are left open, `digest_in_own_group` unwinds them without an Error and keeps the form | open |
| — | subfigure's `\subref{b}` prints "1(b)" where pdflatex prints "(b)": subfigure.sty:412-420 `\subref` reads the `sub@b` label its `\label` writes (the panel's own number), the binding's `\subref` is `\ref` (subfigure_sty.rs, Perl subfigure.sty.ltxml:181 the same); since 78t numbers every panel more papers reach it (review rev78t finding 7). Fix: `\subref` shows the panel's own tag (its `ltx:tag` without the figure's number) | open |
| — | A `\label` after a raw `\refstepcounter` in a paragraph (a user environment's counter, 1505.02540: 8 references print "2", the section's) names the enclosing unit: no schema element there can carry the label and the number (`ltx:para`/`ltx:p` have neither `labels` nor `ltx:tags`). Proposed: `ltx:para` takes `labels` and an optional leading refnum-only `ltx:tags` (the 78g `ltx:picture` mechanism; the XSLT drops `ltx:para/ltx:tags`); reach ≥ 24 papers (78t item 5) | open, awaiting the schema ruling |
| — | algorithm2e sets a caption's text in a box placed at the style's caption position (`\global\sbox\algocf@capbox`, algorithm2e.sty:2305-2336: the bottom for `plain`/`boxed`, the top for the ruled family), so a `\label` in the caption's text is written there, not at its source position: a key labelled in a `plain` algorithm's caption text and again after its last line names the algorithm in pdflatex, the line here (2511.10625 `alg:general_astar`: pdflatex 1, 78t 13; int58a 1). Fix: a caption-text `\label` of an algorithm2e float counts as written at the caption's position (OD #877 residual) | open |
| — | Sub-figures in a `tabular` cell (`\begin{tabular}{ccc}\subfigure[]{…} & \subfigure[]{…} \\ …`) are lifted out of the table and their rows guessed from their widths, not the table's rows (1309.3581, astro-ph0205132: neither the PDF's grid; int58a and 78t alike); their stated widths are not evaluated at the class's real geometry either (`note_stated_widths` does not enter an alignment's cells: 1911.11519 S3.F3, `{subfigure}{\hsize}` in `p{0.45\textwidth}` cells under elsarticle's geometry, 4+3 in the PDF, 3+1+3 here; 2502.05562 S5.F4) | open |
| — | An adjustbox's (or `\resizebox`'s) shell around sub-floats renders a blank oversized row: the sub-floats leave the box (`absorb_figure_material`), and the box stays behind as an `ltx:p` panel holding `<inline-block width="1393.3pt">` with an empty `ltx:p` (review rev78x3 finding 4, probe `q4.tex` Figs 1-2: `\begin{adjustbox}{width=\linewidth,center}` of four sub-figures; the XSLT gives it `style="width:1393.3pt"`, `.ltx_inline-block` is `display:inline-block`, LaTeXML.css:250). Pre-existing (int68a the same for the environment form); 78x keeps the four sub-floats one row after it (`note_panel_hbox`). Direction: drop a box left with no content once its floats are lifted out, or fold its scale onto them | open |
| — | A raster graphic's explicit size comes out at 72/72.27 of it, rounded up to a whole pixel: `image_graphicx_sizer`'s pixel branch (`latexml_core/src/util/image.rs`, `apply_graphicx_ops` with `units_per_bp = DPI/72.27`) multiplies graphicx's bp values (`parse_graphicx_options`, `to_bp`) by pixels per pt, as Perl does (Util/Image.pm:162-163 `to_bp`, :237 `ceil($a1 * $dppt)` with `$dppt = DPI/72.27`; a Perl-origin defect, KNOWN_PERL_ERRORS #1142): `\includegraphics[width=114.6pt,height=1cm]{x.png}` is 114.187pt (158px) where TeX's box is 114.6pt, so two such panels stay one row of REVTeX `[preprint,twocolumn]`'s 229pt column where pdflatex breaks them (review rev78x3 finding 6; probes `rvpt.tex`, `rvpt2.tex`). The vector branch is TeX's (`graphicx_box_pt`, 78z). Direction: an explicit `width=`/`height=` is the box's size in pt, as TeX's; reach every raster with a stated size (box and row widths shift by ≤ 0.4 %) | open |
| — | `\adjustbox{max width=\columnwidth}{\subfloat[a]{…}\subfloat[b]{…}}` in a float (revtex4-1 twocolumn): the two sub-figures land outside the scaled `ltx:inline-block`, which keeps an empty `<p><text/></p>`, and come out `P \| PP` where pdflatex sets one line holding one adjustbox; the figure counter steps twice, so the next float is numbered one too high (refnums `5`, `5a`… where pdflatex's `.aux` reads `\numberline {4}`). int69b, 78x r4 and r5 alike (pre-existing): adjustbox digests its content twice — fix in the adjustbox/subcaption interaction. Review rev78x5 finding 1, probe `rev78x5/p/m3.tex` (third figure) | OPEN (RED) |
| — | Sub-figures lifted out of their `figure*` into a paragraph of floats settled into a row (`settle_float_paragraph`) beside the float's minipage remnant (2202.03884 S4.SS1.fig1: `{subfigure}{0.33\columnwidth}`s with `{center}` and `\resizebox` bodies around pgfplots `\input`s; the PDF 2+1, int67a 2+2, 78x r2 one per row, 78x r3 as int68a): the lift is the defect (the sub-figures belong to S4.F5); the row is set at the registers' widths (OD #885) | open |
| — | Rows TeX keeps tight or overfull (a `~` tie, `}\subfloat` with no breakpoint, tight TikZ, two 4.5cm sub-floats in a 252pt column, a nested `\subfloat` beside a 4.5cm one with ~1pt to spare in a 246pt column — base the same, review rev78x5 finding 2, `rev78x5/p/m3.tex` fourth figure): 1108.5543 Fig 9, 2009.04593 Fig 5, 1712.01536 Fig 2, 1703.09165 (2 floats), 2406.05437 Fig 12; and the `figure_mixed_content` fixture's 0.56/0.42 minipages with `\hspace{8px}` (8.03pt), one overfull line in pdflatex (`\showbox`: both in one `\hbox`, glue set −1.0), two rows under the rule (review rev78x finding 10); and a subfigure.sty body whose stated `\hspace` makes its row overfull (two `\subfigure[]{\includegraphics[width=0.47\textwidth]{…}\hspace{0.5cm}}`: pdflatex one overfull line, glue set −1.0; int69b one row, 78x r4 two, the body's glue counted: review rev78x4 `n4.tex` F4) | accepted residual (user 2026-10-22: overflow) |
| — | **Panel grid markup** (grid design payload 3): the XML says a float's rows only implicitly (the runs between `ltx:break`s); missing are a panel's stated width (Perl subcaption.sty.ltxml:60-66 stores `width` and never emits it), spans outside a `tabular`, the column a panel takes across rows, gallery or matrix (int61a sets 1 of 10 labelled matrices as the PDF). Options (study Q4): (a) today's elements plus attributes — `panelwidth`/`panelspan` on a panel, `panelrows`/`panelcols` and `ltx_panels_gallery`/`ltx_panels_matrix` on the float (LaTeXML.model `figure_attributes`; the XSLT maps them to CSS custom properties as `@backgroundcolor`); (b) a row wrapper element; (c) a table grid. Sizing S1/S2/S3, a two-column class's `\columnwidth`, matrix detection by heuristic or `tabular` only (design study task #360); CSS in LaTeXML.css and ar5iv-css at the goal's end | RULED 2026-10-22: today's elements plus attributes (`panelwidth`/`panelspan` on panels, `panelrows`/`panelcols` + `ltx_panels_gallery`/`ltx_panels_matrix` on the float, XSLT → CSS custom properties); sizing keeps the panels' relative sizes within a figure (no per-image cap that skews them — precomputed sizing values as CSS custom properties; design proposal owed) — after the grid rule payload. MARKUP FIXED (79c, OD #887): `annotate_panel_grid` (after `arrange_panels` and `collapse_float`) states `panelrows`/`panelcols`/the class on floats of 2+ panels, `panelwidth` where the width is the real geometry's and stated (`panel_width_is_stated`; never a guess), a matrix only on a `tabular` of panels or 2+ rows agreeing panel for panel (widths within 0.01 of the line: 0.0-0.01 score alike on the oracle, 0.02 adds a false matrix) with spanning rows, and sub-captions on none of the panels or on exactly one whole row (79c r2); nested groups their own; schema `Panel.attributes` in `Common.attributes` (panels are any element) and `PanelGrid.attributes` on figure/table/float; XSLT `--ltx-panel-rows/cols` on the float's element, `--ltx-panel-width/span` on its `div.ltx_flex_cell`. Oracle (453): study matrix 42 → ours 20 matrix / 22 gallery (16 text-labelled matrices), study gallery 411 → 405 gallery / 5 matrix (grids captioned on one row only) / 1 none; rows 429, shapes 422 unchanged. A/B int71a → 79c (1,907): E 6,680 = 6,680, F 20 = 20, W 59,351 = 59,351, words equal; with the markup stripped 1,855 papers byte-identical, the 52 others the label item's (row above). Sizing RULED 2026-10-23: the float states `panelline` (its line ÷ the PDF's running-text column) and `panelfill` (widest row's Σ `panelwidth`); a panel renders at `panelwidth × panelline` of the theme column ÷ max(1, `panelfill × panelline`); no CSS rule reads them yet (goal-end CSS). 79c r2 (review rev79c): a `tabular` cell counts only for a graphic 24pt+ wide and tall (13 data tables lose the matrix), sub-captions allow a matrix only on exactly one whole row (6 partial-caption grids → galleries), a beside-content collapse drops the inner's widths, a `width=`-only graphic panel states its stated width, the grid attributes typed numeric and the XSLT emits numbers only; guards `perfect_kernel_batch79c::*`, `integration::panel_grid_markup_states_only_numbers`. Residuals: text-labelled matrices (label rows are `ltx:p` panels), a `\resizebox` of measured content states no width (its stated width is not on the element), a raster inside a sub-float body keeps KPE #1142's rounding in its sub-float's width; a float held in a minipage — FIXED 79f (the page outside the box, 0.5 as the PDF); a `\captionof` figure gets no grid markup (not a float environment; Perl alike); class pages declared — FIXED 79f (OD #887). LANDED int74 (61039b657e + 140d474145) |
| — | The natural `cssstyle` is the size pdflatex prints a graphic at (`first_natural_size`, util/image.rs; 79a), but post renders the candidate it finds most desirable (`latexml_post/src/graphics.rs:321-371`), which may be another file of another shape: 2107.02597's gnuplot figures (`\DeclareGraphicsExtensions{.pdf,.eps,.png,.jpg,.jpeg}`, a 640x480 png beside each eps) render the png (4:3) at the eps's `36.135em x 25.294em` (aspect 1.43), about 7 % out of proportion (int68a the same, `cssstyle` A = B). Options: post renders the candidate the box is sized from when it can convert it (an eps through gs), or the `cssstyle` keeps the box's width and lets the height follow the rendered file. Review rev79a finding 5 | open |
| — | `\Gin@extensions` is seeded with pdftex.def's list (`graphics_sty.rs`, pdftex.def:597-600) under every driver option, though `\Gin@driver` names the option's driver (`graphics_driver()`): `\usepackage[dvips]{graphicx}` loads dvips.def in TeX, whose list is `.eps,.ps,.eps.gz,.ps.gz,.eps.Z,.mps` (dvips.def:202), so pdflatex takes `q.eps` (40.15pt) beside `q.png` where ours (int68a, 79a) takes the png (144.54pt); likewise `[dvipdfmx]`/`[xetex]`. Fix: seed by `graphics_driver()` from each driver file's list. A corpus-wide shift for every `[dvips]` paper with a raster and a vector of one basename: its own A/B. Review rev79a2 finding 3, probe `/home/deyan/.claude/jobs/356a0c47/tmp/rev79a2/g/gh.tex` | open (ranked: after the panel grid payloads) |

Witness cond-mat0610021 (`bb=0in 0in 2in 2in`; Fig 11's sub-figure then breaks where TeX sets one overfull `\hbox`). Review rev78z finding 5 | OPEN |
| — | A float LaTeX defers writes its `\newlabel` when placed, so a later `\label` of the same key loses to it: `\begin{figure}[p]\caption{Deferred}\label{k}\end{figure}\section{Two}\label{k}See \ref{k}.` — pdflatex "See 1.", ours, int58a and Perl "See 2." (review rev78t2's lb2; OD #877, KPE #1131); rare | open |
| — | An author's mark whose label holds any control sequence prints nothing: the mark reader reads the label from the operand's unexpanded text (`author_macros.rs:198` `clean_frontmatter_labels(&operand.to_string(),…)`), which no list answers, so `\textsuperscript{\relax 3}` loses its "3" (pdflatex "Ann Alpha3"), `\textsuperscript{\one}` and `\textsuperscript{\protect 2}` their "1" and "2", `\textsuperscript{\relax 1\thanks{a}}` and `\textsuperscript{\protect 2\thanks{b}}` keep the note but lose the label, as do `1\protect\protect\thanks{b}`, `{\protect}1\thanks{f}` and `\protect\protect\thanks{c}`, and `1,\protect\one\thanks{n}` gives `<sup>1</sup>` where pdflatex prints "1,1". No error is reported. int62a, int63a and 78v alike (older than 78v); synthetic, no census paper. Fix: read the label from what it typesets (expand macros, drop an unexpandable `\relax`/`\protect`) before matching it. Review rev78v2 finding 6a, widened by rev78v3 finding 3 (probes `rev78v3/q/pl.tex`, `pra.tex`, `prc.tex`, `rev78v2/p/sh3.tex`); RED repro `sectioning-frontmatter/author_mark_relax_before_its_label` | open |
| — | A note macro reached through four macros (`\fnM→\fnN→\fnO→\fnP{8\footnotemark}`) inside an author's mark loses both its "8" and the footnotemark (pdflatex "Di Delta8†"): `makes_a_note`/`prints_beside_a_note` (author_block/preprocess.rs) look three deep at most, so the macro is neither taken out nor read in place and stays in a label nothing answers. int62a, int63a and 78v alike; synthetic. Fix: past the depth bound, fall back to taking the macro out whole (the int62a behaviour for a note macro). Review rev78v2 finding 6b; RED repro `sectioning-frontmatter/author_mark_four_deep_note_macro_chain` | open |
| — | Author marks: inside a note macro's body, a `\protect` before another note-making macro (`\newcommand\fnL{\footnotemark}`, `\newcommand\fnB{7\protect\fnL}`, `Lu L\textsuperscript{\fnB}`) is not label-safe, so the macro is taken out whole and its "7" lands in the name text: `L7[mark]` where 78v r2 gave `L[mark]<sup>7</sup>` (pdflatex "L7§"). `prints_beside_a_note` (`author_block/preprocess.rs:233-236`) lets a `\protect` through only before a note command, where `split_mark_notes` (`:105-108`) also takes one before a note-making macro. Markup only, nothing printed lost; 78v r3 = int63a; synthetic, no census paper. Fix: accept a `\protect` before a `makes_a_note` macro in the body loop as `split_mark_notes` does, a row in `perfect_kernel_batch78v::author_protect_thanks_and_note_macro_inside_a_mark`, OD #881 "before a note command or note macro". Review rev78v3 finding 1 (probe `rev78v3/q/prb.tex`) | open |
| — | A formula silently degrades to `ltx_math_unparsed` under load: 1001.0023 `S9.E2.m1.pic1.m2` once in a census run (FM r9 writer's set-mode census), never in 3+1+2+2 reruns; the log identical, so no warning — the math parser's 2 s pruned-only budget (`parser.rs:235`) is the only time-based path found (revfms2a2_9 finding 7). A time budget that changes output without a diagnostic hides regressions in A/Bs: emit a Warning when the budget cuts a parse, or make the cut deterministic | open |
| — | **Complete breakage at 100 % (user 2026-10-24)**: a missing image, a `\ref` or `\cite` with no target, where pdflatex resolves it. Method: a fresh sample per round (`one_side.sh`, `scratch_356a0c47/brk<NN>/`), `scratch_356a0c47/fms2b_census/scan.py` over the core XML (a `graphics` with no `candidates`, a `labelref` with no label, a `bibref` with no `bibitem` — skip `show="nothing"`), then `classify.py` against the source (a file of that name shipped, a `\label`/`\bibitem` in a file this document reads — strip comments and post-`\end{document}` text, exclude xr\'s other documents); a label a macro makes classifies UNDEF, so read those too. Not ours (pdflatex or the arXiv PDF breaks too): a file not shipped, xr without the other `.aux`, a stale shipped `.bbl` (ruling 2026-10-24: 2509.06285, 2205.10368, 2402.06576, 2311.01729, 2205.00263, 2004.00833, 2507.01293, 1806.02261), the harness\'s main-file guess, a document\'s own `\def\label`, a shipped namesake of a TL package (ruling 2026-10-17 H1: 0802.3014\'s and 1210.0342\'s pretex `units.sty`). | — | **Frontmatter architecture: the audit's plan** (`parity/FRONTMATTER_ARCHITECTURE_AUDIT_2026-10-28.md`; the user's principle 2026-10-28: bindings only through the Frontmatter API, general reorganizing abstractions at every stage, special cases local in bindings or behind API opt-in data): the pipeline is Perl's (queue → digest → place) and the Rust-only API extensions are opt-in data (`owner=sequence`, `_share`, `\lx@title@inst`); ranked work — (1) the observer policy table keyed on binding file names in generic code (`observer/policy.rs:98-260`, `written: true` for revtex/ams) becomes binding-declared conventions; (2) one kill switch per general reorganizer (86g cover order, #262 placement, #246 wrap, `distribute_upfront_contacts`, `respell_emails`, K11 reroute, deposit keep), defaults unchanged, for exact A/B attribution; (3) an author-convention registry merging the ~7 drifted vocabulary lists in `author_block/` (llncs `\inst`/`\fnmsep`, ACM `\alignauthor`, IJCAI `\affiliations`, IEEE `\IEEEauthorrefmark`, `\thanksref`, `\altaffilmark`), bindings declaring their entries; (4) 86g: a sentence-length `\noindent` paragraph is not cover unless deposit-stamped (about 39 of the 295 covers rest on `ltx_noindent` alone; a CONFIRMED body-prose probe in the audit); (5) contrib bindings building `<ltx:classification>`/`<ltx:abstract>`/contacts in place (siamart, interact, jmlr, jmlr2e, ieeeojcsys, ieeetaes, agujournal2019, optica_article, dmtcs_episciences; svmult `\lx@sv@chapauthor`) go through the API; (6) the IJCAI sectioned split becomes a generic `\lx@add@authors@sectioned`; (7) a shared `frontmatter::tokens` toolkit for the six contrib bindings that re-implement the reader's primitives (ptephy, osa_journal, epl, eptcs, aistats, interspeech), as each is touched; (8) record `distribute_upfront_contacts`' grid as charter §4's list evidence or gate it; (9) one ownership rule with one home — DECIDED (user 2026-10-28: expand the home that started as the general layer): the observer (OD #837, FM S2/K23, "a single resolver" over what TeX prints) is the single home; no new ownership rule goes into `author_block/`, `shared_row` or the insert passes; the old reader keeps what feeds the events and retires per the S2 inventory; 85a's and 85b's rules are ported into the resolver when `article` and llncs reach S2c; item (1) — binding-declared conventions instead of the name-keyed policy table — comes first; **item (1) DONE (87a)**; item (7) WITHDRAWN 2026-10-29 by the digested-representation refactor (next row): each binding declares its conventions at load (`declare_frontmatter_conventions`, its `FRONTMATTER_CONVENTIONS`), the observer reads them from the state, `KERNEL` for a family that declares none; the table is gone (OD #837 "The conventions' home"; a pure refactor: census records and A/B unchanged; guard `perfect_kernel_batch87a::*`)  **Item 9 DECIDED by the user 2026-11-01 and made the frontmatter priority:** "keep evolving our design of the Frontmatter API until it can accommodate all variations used in arXiv. Prioritize refactoring to a better model of a single ownership engine, unified machinery and API, unified placement order inside the API" — one ownership engine (reader + insert-time passes and the observer converge), every binding's placement through the shared machinery (no binding-private splits: llncs, sv_support, inst_support, aa, ptephy, webofc), one placement order inside the API (marks/labels → the name a contact spells → the class's declared convention for unmarked lines → the block), bindings declaring conventions only (the ~95 per-binding `annotate=` modes: run 42, new 35, all 16, last 1, N 1). Failures of the old design that motivated it: rev97cd C1 (sv_support's split put an institute's e-mail on every author: 2101.04527, 2206.03859, 2404.15833) and F1 (aastex `annotate=run` overriding marks: 1810.01941 39 → 364 affiliations). Design study done: the plan is `parity/FRONTMATTER_SINGLE_ENGINE_PLAN.md` (the observer emits owners at the flush as Perl's own `label=`/`annotations=` keys instead of patching the old reader's DOM; Stages 0-7; rulings R1-R9); its stages are the batches after int126a. | OPEN (plan) |

Respell rule measured on real papers (OD #938): today's (targets by the printed names, base letters only veto) stays — the rival (FIDELITY_CHARTER #420 read literally: an own one-name, one-address call never respelled) is wrong on 22 of the 24 papers where they differ in a 347-paper respell population (corresponding addresses after the last `\author`: 1303.5460, 2005.06651, 1511.04148, 2408.09634); **OPEN, RED** where today's is wrong: `amsart_email_of_a_two_name_call_spelling_an_accented_name` (2112.10604, `mathiae@` on Thiel: base-letter targets for a several-name call alone would fix it) and `revtex_email_spelling_its_holders_given_name` (0711.3633, 0809.3090: `santos@` moved off Santos Bravo Yuste; a given-name veto would fix it), and the ASCII twin `\author{Jorg Mueller}` (moves, FIDELITY_CHARTER §4 sentence corrected for the main session's confirmation). | — | **Compound marks: one rule for the superscript path, the formula path's runs and the legend's label (94a r9 residual; OD #928 round 9, OD #927)**: the three readers part a mark of several symbols differently — the superscript path at commas only (`apply::show_legend_mark`'s `ltx:sup` loop: `Ann Able$^{*\dagger}$` under a `$^{*}$Corresponding author` legend shows "*†" beside the note's ∗, the star twice, once as ASCII `*`; w01), the formula path by `token_runs` and `repeated_symbols` (`$^{1,*\spadesuit}$`'s ∗ taken with the comma before it, "1♠"), the reading's labels by `Entry::compound` and `mark:parted` (`**` read as `*,*`, so a `$^*$` legend is the `**` carrier's, w11; the kernel then hides the `**`: `\author{Ann Able$^{1,**}$, Bob Baker$^{1}$\\ $^1$Univ X\\ $^*$Corresponding author}` prints "1,∗∗", ours no mark, as 93a), and a `$^{*\dagger}$Corresponding author` legend reaches Bob Baker, who carries only `*` (`Ann Able$^{1,*\dagger}$ and Bob Baker$^{1,*}$`, w10, since 93a). Constraints any rule keeps: 2602.19190 (a run of distinct symbols labels a line as a whole), 2606.15609 (`$^{*\spadesuit}$` requests each symbol), a repeated symbol a mark of its own (94a r9 guard `a_repeated_symbol_beside_a_legend_is_its_own_mark`). A partial take also drops the comma before the taken symbol: `$^{1,**\dagger}$` under a `**` legend gives "1†" where pdflatex prints "1,∗∗†", and `$^{1,\dagger\ddagger}$` under `†` gives "1‡". Remove the comma only when the whole comma-delimited run is taken (`apply.rs` ~l.258; rev94a r9 r12). RED catalog repros: `author_names_compound_mark_superscript_beside_its_legend`, `author_names_compound_legend_goes_to_a_partial_carrier`, `author_names_kernel_repeated_symbol_beside_a_legend_hidden` (the kernel path's `**` hidden; rev94a r9 a03, siblings a01, a05, a10, a11) | OPEN (refactor) |

| — | **Frontmatter notes read as the class prints them; a note with no ink is none (92e, 92g; user rulings 2026-10-30, FIDELITY_CHARTER §2, §4)** (OD #915, #916, #921): fixed by `name=` where the class prints another label — aa/svjour `\offprints` "Send offprint requests to:", jheppub `\arxivnumber` "ArXiv ePrint:", acmart `\acmISBN` "ACM ISBN", iopart `\submitto`/`\submitted` "Submitted to:", AASTeX `\submitjournal` "Submitted to", IEEEoj/ieeetj `\doiinfo` and old LIPIcs `\DOI` "Digital Object Identifier"; a note is dropped only when it provably prints nothing — every item known blank (`leaves_no_ink`: white space, glue, kerns, breaks, struts, phantoms, an unframed box or a constructor whose literal template prints only its arguments, over blank arguments) —, emptied by us (`\offprints{\email{…}}`: astro-ph0405280, astro-ph0608669) or by the author (`\thanks{}`, `\externaleditor{~}`: 2004.01582, 2401.15379, 2609.00671); any doubt is ink (a constructor's own text, `\openone`, `\dots`, `\LaTeX`, `$\vdots$`; an element of its own; a closure or a construction hook, `\emph{}`, `\url{}`; a sized rule, a frame, a leader, an error marker; a background colour, `\colorbox{red}{}`), and the observer drops the same notes on the same input (`ruling4:inkless`; a note's text is no part of the block's empty-mark filter). Blank notes the test keeps: catalog repro `kernel_thanks_blank_notes_kept`. **Fixed by 92g (OD #921; user rulings 2026-10-30, FIDELITY_CHARTER §4: kept, with the label the class prints — none —, the role kept as `role=` and `ltx_role_<role>`; an explicit empty `name=` is no label, a forwarded empty optional label keeps the role's)** — the notes whose class prints NO label, now none: the kernel's title `\thanks{X}` "Thanks: X" (pdflatex a "∗ X" footnote; amsppt `\thanks…\endthanks`, amsppt.sty:373; IEEEtran `\thanksto`); "Dedication:" for aa (aa.cls:596-600), svjour3 (:612-616), jheppub (:225), JHEP (:608), jinstpub (:237), elsart (:1165), amsppt `\dedicatory` (:504), tac (:311), compositio (:244), epl2 (:537), wileymsp — all italic, no label (dgruyter left: it prints "Dedicated to X" under article only (dgruyter.sty:2964, "Für" in ngerman; a page elsewhere, :3128)); "Preprint:" for report numbers set top right (REVTeX's printed `\preprint`, aps4-2.rtx:250-264; svjour.cls:489-492 `\preprint`, jheppub.sty:71/166, JHEP.cls:390, JINST, aastex631 `\reportnum` :651, atlasdoc :806/835, appolb's running head :163, jinstpub's header :178); "Note:" for aa `\headnote` (:569), acmart `\titlenote`/`\subtitlenote` (footnotes), aastex/aguplus/emulateapj `\slugcomment`, ijcai `\pubnote` (running head, ijcai24.sty:62), pos.sty `\notes`; "Journal:" for aa/svjour `\journalname` (printed "X manuscript no.", aa.cls:541, svjour3.cls:536: a suffix, not a label), acmart `\acmJournal`, emulateapj/aastex `\journalinfo` (textsc, :402/:694), apa7 `\journal` (header, apa7.cls:1456), arximspdf, mn2e ("Mon. Not. R. Astron. Soc. V, pages", mn2e.cls:1080), aastex.cls `\journal`/`\volume`/`\issue` (gobbled, aastex.cls:1893-1895: not printed at all); "Conference:" for jheppub `\proceeding` (:172), JHEP/JINST `\conference`, acmart `\acmConference` (bibstrip "In …"); "DOI:" for acmart/aamas `\acmDOI` (printed as the https://doi.org/ URL) and pnas `\doi` (footer); the volume for apa7, ams_support `\issueinfo` (read "Volume: Volume 12, Number 3, …"; amsart.cls:121-143 prints `\volinfo` alone); "PubID:" for IEEEtran `\IEEEpubid` (read "PubID: pubid: X", Perl's content, now X alone as IEEEtran.cls:6153 prints it), PoS `\PoS` (watermark), apa7 `\copnum`; "Publication type:" for iopart `\article`'s type (a heading, iopart.cls:148), svjour `\papertype` (not printed), elsart `\articletype`. The class's own label instead: acmart `\acmVolume` "Vol." (acmart.cls:3088, the journal formats' foot), arximspdf `\volume` "Vol.", `\issue` "No." (:366, :383). Witnesses e1-e6 of the 92e rerun: article, IEEEtran, amsart, emulateapj, aa, aastex631; guards `perfect_kernel_batch92g::*`. **Version-dependent**: elsarticle `\journal` — copies up to v3.3 print "Preprint submitted to X" (2609.40131's v1.21 :453), TL 2025's v3.4c prints no journal line (removed 2024-10-22, elsarticle.cls:1043-1045); elsart "Preprint submitted to X" unless `\volume` is set ("Article published in …", elsart.cls:1203-1218). Labels already the class's: aa `\doi` "DOI:" (aa.cls:363, modern :430), atlasdoc DOI (:794), imsart `\arxiv` "arXiv:" (imsart.sty:1167). **Out of scope, with 92c**: the e-mail inside an `\offprints` note — aa/svjour print "…: O. Kochukhov, e-mail: oleg@x.se" in the note, our `\email` is the current creator's contact, so the note reads "…: O. Kochukhov," and the address sits on the last author named before it; placing it by the note's name waits for aa/llncs as written families (92a's observer reads the owner from the note's text) | LANDED 92e, 92g; version-dependent ones open |
| — | **An unmarked address after a name list goes to the last name only (iopart, informs4; pre-existing, Perl's default)**: `\author{A, B and C}\address{X}` (iopart) and `\AUTHOR{A, B}\AFF{X}` (informs4) give X to C (B) alone, where the print sets X under all of them. Review rev100c fixes (r2): (B1) the re-part declines a made item that joins two or more Name events of the block one of which is a whole row (a line, a cell, a spaced-apart piece — no comma's piece) that may on its own name a person (`repart.rs` `joined_rows`/`whole_row`; 2609.37429's `\FormatAuthor` pair, Zihan Hu inside Chiesa's affiliation — today's one creator again; RED repro `boxed_block_two_people_in_one_cell`); (S1) on a line of notes only, only the notes a name's mark requests join the names, the others are taken out of the block as document pubnotes (`preprocess.rs` `notes_the_names_request`, `\thanks` → `\lx@add@pubnote@thanks`), the resolver reads them the block's (`note:unrequested`, `resolver/blocks.rs`) and the writer holds such a block note where today's prints the pubnote (`plan::held_as_pubnote`): 2609.17106's funding note, 2312.10807's `$^{*}$`/`$^{\dagger}$` legends (no longer Alois Knoll's), a `$^{\dagger}$` in such a note no name's mark (review probe q11). Guards `perfect_kernel_batch100c::{a_note_no_name_requests_stays_the_documents, a_person_in_a_row_an_item_joins_is_never_a_contact}`. RESIDUALS, pre-existing, base too: the re-part's written affiliations drop the block's `\itshape` (base keeps `<text font="italic">` where the re-part does not run); an e-mail row continuing a marked legend line becomes a creator with no name (RED `marked_legend_email_row_is_no_creator`, sibling of 3B); a marked "John Smith Laboratory" affiliation merges the names with a `merged_creators` Error (RED `marked_affiliation_named_laboratory_merges_names`). **Open:** (cells) the resolver's `joins_previous` (`resolver/lines.rs:668`, rule `name:layout-joined`) joins unmarked names in adjacent `tabular` columns into one name (`Ann Able & \mbox{\ \ } & Bob Baker`, 1302.4168; also the affiliations below them, `Univ A & Univ B`), so the block stays one creator (RED repro `boxed_block_names_in_two_columns`); a cell boundary should part names (EASY in the resolver; changes readings beyond the boxed blocks, so its own A/B). (3B, deferred) an e-mail no name spells in a boxed block is a block-level contact (FIDELITY_CHARTER §4, 2026-11-01), which the writer has no form for short of OD #159's nameless creator — the re-part declines ("a block e-mail no name spells"), today's one creator stays (2605.09461, 2609.36484; RED repro `boxed_block_emails_no_name_spells`); needs a block-level contact in the writer's `dom::Front` (only creators today) and the API. (exposed) the e-mail item lifts the re-part's last decline on boxed blocks, so readings that decline masked now ship (no content lost, the scorer better or equal on each): 2609.37429 (`\FormatAuthor{name}{mail}{inst}` nested `tabular`s side by side — the second author read as a continued line of the first's affiliation, "EPFL Zihan Hu zihan.hu@epfl.ch EPFL"; declined since r2 by `joined_rows`, the reading's continuation across two nested `tabular`s in one cell still open), 2603.19216 (planstyle's `\affil` after a boxed `\author`: the reading's `api:annotate:run` gives it to the last name only; 7 names now right, 6 without the shared affiliation), 1302.4168 (names joined across cells, now declined); proposed one fail-safe for the main session: a boxed block's re-part writes only when every Name event of the block that reads as a person on its own (`may_name_a_person`) is exactly one reading creator's name — never part of another name or of an item (it would subsume `joined_across_cells`). (Kind) `\IEEEauthorrefmark{n}` prints the n-th footnote symbol (∗, †, …; IEEEtran.cls:4528-4530 `\@fnsymbol`), but the reader keys it as `n`, so legend notes `\thanks{$^*$Equal contribution}` / `$^\dagger$Corresponding…` answer no request (2312.10807: document pubnotes since r2, no longer the last author's); the charter's printed-label rule wants the symbol key, which would make them the refmarked authors' legends. | — | **Token-stack RAII (int131fix follow-up)** — `invoke_token` (stomach.rs) pushes `token_stack` and pops it on its normal exits only; the recovery arms now clear the stack (`stomach::clear_token_stack`), but an error caught below them (a speculative digestion that discards its `Err`, a binding's `catch`) still leaves its entries, and a later digestion can trip `MAXSTACK` early with a ghost `Stomach:Recursion`. Fix: an RAII pop guard beside `local_current_token_guard`, every exit of `invoke_token` popping once. Witnesses 2410.07647/2105.02599 (fixed by the clear); 2502.10606 (a speculative title-page run's discarded `Stomach:Recursion`, 106s at int135b) fixed by `stomach::StomachMark`, which the speculative runs now take and put back (row 106s) — a binding's own `catch` is still uncovered. At each `\maketitle` the deposit runs the whole title page once more as pdflatex runs it (sect05.rs `note_title_page_print`: the class's refused `\maketitle` or `\@maketitle`, or LaTeX's over `\@title`/`\@author`/`\@date` then `\@thanks`; `\today` empty, `\thanks` latex.ltx's — its text printed only where `\@thanks` runs — unless a raw file defined its own, in a `FieldLedgerScope`), read by `ink_word_text` (util_text.rs); when the document closes (`title_page_check.rs` `check_title_page_words`) each content word of the print the output does not hold (text, attributes but `tex`/`text`/id, pieces joined across element edges, `--streaming` spills through the spill observer into a per-conversion thread-local set of distinct words, cut to the print's once it printed) and the document's source writes (`binding::content::CONTENT_FILES` and the source directory's files read as definitions, their definitions' bodies out; keys and options out; Latin-1 by fallback) is named in one `Warning:frontmatter:unclaimed`; a class's own word (label, placeholder at its default) is an Info `frontmatter:class_text` (placeholder ruling pending). **Coverage** (int133a's 56 dev20k + 42 long-tail lost-words papers): 1 → 24 name their lost words (22 all, 2 some; dev 15, long tail 9); silent 74 = 19 scorer artefacts (census132 §3) + 15 with no print run (a binding's own `\maketitle`: daj 1510.08733, 2112.09352, elsarticle 2104.14704, aastex7/701 2606.23168, 2607.00453, econsocart 2203.00070; | — | **106t: the title-page check's second source — what TeX makes of what the document gave its frontmatter commands (Bundle 4; OD #953 "106t")** — DONE (r2, rule (A): digest, never read tokens): a check, the XML the base's. A class binding that writes its own frontmatter has no print run, and a binding's command may drop what pdflatex prints. The calls of LaTeXML's macros read from the document's own text (`Expandable::watched_call` → `state::note_document_call`, each argument with whether the body names it) inside which the frontmatter API queues an entry (`title_page_check::note_frontmatter_entry`) are its frontmatter commands; wanted beside the print, all digested: the entry's own digested content (`end_frontmatter_entry_digestion`, `ink_word_text`), and, digested once speculatively (sect05.rs `speculative_word_run`), a required argument the command's body never names (quantumarticle `\ead`, KPE #1208), an internal command's optional one (`\begin{abstract}[Summary]`, KPE #1209) and the document's text a binding-made macro drops while the entry digests (`state::note_dropped_argument`, filtered to what the document wrote: IEEEtran's `\lx@IEEE@membership@drop`, jmlr's `\titletag`). A binding's by-design drop is declared on its own definition, `DefMacro!(…, drops => [n])` (`Expandable::dropped_by_design`, the expansion unchanged), only where the real file prints nothing of the argument or prints it as page furniture: hyperref `\texorpdfstring` #2 (2306.06741, 2306.06006), dmtcs `\publicationdetails` #5, lipics `\volumeinfo`, lmcs `\lmcsheading`, aomart `\subject` #2, midl `\orcid`, cas `\corref`, icml/OmniBus `\comment` (main-session ruling (b)). r1's 42 token-reading false alarms pass unchanged; two guards re-pinned to true losses pdflatex prints (`batch86i` amsart `[Résumé]`, `batch86d` the pinned residual's IEEE grade). **Coverage** (98 lost-words papers): 24 → 28 (26 all, 2 some: 2209.07123, 2104.14704, 1511.03982, 2211.07413); r1's token reading named 4 more that digestion cannot (2110.09105 aa `\email jose…` an undelimited argument; 1107.2462, 2101.10506, 2203.00070 raw classes with no kept print run — 106s's reach). **False alarms:** 0 over a 500-paper bound-class control from `popint125a_s10k` and 500 random. (1) `NameKeys::of`, the one place every spelling site reads, keys a name by its base letters and by `resolver::transliteration` (ä ae, ö oe, ü ue, ß ss, å aa, ø oe, æ ae, œ oe), an accent or letter command read as the letter it prints (`spelling_name_text`); callers pass the name unfolded; `respell_emails` moves an e-mail by the name as printed or transliterated, never base-folded (`NameKeys::as_printed`; user ruling 2026-11-05: `joerg.mueller@` is Jörg Müller's, so charter §4 #420's `mueller-group@` example moves to him, `muller-group@` stays); a word's length class is its base spelling's, a literal-vs-transliterated tie stays a tie (`mueller@` beside Hans Mueller and Jörg Müller: the block; user ruling 2026-11-05), and a surname's six-letter prefix never reuses the given name's letters (review rev107a; the last also fixes a pre-existing `andrea.rossi@` on Andrea Andreassen). (2) The flush's Kind step for an address line reads a `{…}@domain` list set over several lines as one line (a break inside a printed brace, or between the brace and its `@domain`; entry lines joined; the list's lines the author block made contacts of their own joined, `join_split_address_contacts`), placed as the one-line form (charter §4's order, `email_owners::list_owners`): the author an entry spells, else the consistent author-order list (OD #805; its own guard the synthetic `email_list_split_order_only`), else the only author (2311.03226), else elimination (77p r4; 1808.00588's `kdm@`, 2305.13191's `wshui@`/`drot@`/`ballemig@`), else the OD #159 block creator; a declined split list is one counted `Warning:frontmatter:address-list`, and an `@` with no dotted domain after it is no list. A/B `pop107a.tsv` vs int138a (1,404): 20 frontmatter changes, all better (14 transliterated e-mails placed, 6 split lists read), E/F equal, `fm_checks.py` 1-7 clean; re-score 5 creators right, 5 "wrong" by scorer limits (1808.00588, 2407.04582). Round 2 (final) vs int138a: 23 papers changed, all better; vs round 1: 5, all better — 2305.13191 and 1808.00588 placed by elimination, and three e-mails moved by the transliteration ruling (1502.02658 `akuepper@` → Küpper, astro-ph0611772 `cthoene@` → Thöne, hep-th9710082 `c.koehl@` → Köhl); a literal-vs-transliterated tie stays a tie (user 2026-11-05). Pereira"; int124b and r3 alike), and the reading numbers them as entry 1. sv_support is written since 97e (OD #948); the writer declines an affiliation writing one of the paper's names, or printing another author's name, that the reading gives an author (`writer::plan::{writes_the_name, prints_the_name}`; 2609.05466, 1506.02396, 1908.03628), so today's still prints. | — | **95b r3 review should-fixes (rev95b-r3 SHIP, 2026-10-31; none a regression or a loss)** — (S1) a setter's input mark can stay open after its expansion ends: `state.rs` `input_unread_since` (~:2436-2449) compares the mouth's depth and read position but not which mouth, and token-list digestion runs on `Mouth::default()` (position (0,0,0,0), mouth.rs:164-175), so a mark taken at the end of a digested list reads open in a later default-mouth context; it can only cost a write (a written item kept as today's, or the paper declined), never write an unwritten family's item; fix: a per-mouth serial in `InputMark` (the per-context `ctx_serial`, gullet.rs:4936-4941); unconfirmed (probe: svjour3 + authblk, `\email{..}` last in a digested argument, a later unwatched API call, the `.fmshadow.json` `binding`). (S2) the watch switches on in any written-family paper that defines one of the setter names itself — the `document`/`fallback` owner counts as an unwritten family (`ledger.rs` `watch_unwritten_setters` ~:494-505): article + authblk + `\newcommand{\email}[1]{\texttt{#1}}` (review probe `rev95b3/q1`, control `q2`) costs +0.28 % instructions and marks earlier calls `unobserved`; creators unchanged; fix: exclude non-binding owners from the trigger, or correct the comment and OD #942. (S5) the census ran in default mode, so it cannot count papers where the watch was on: 9 of the 419 mixed papers have authblk under an unwritten class (2005.13982, 2106.12864, 2108.03328, 2110.11194, 2211.06456, 2305.02433, 2311.07372, 2502.07150, 2609.05466) — re-run them with `LATEXML_FM_SHADOW=1`. Review `agent_reports/2026-10-31_rev95b_r3.md` | OPEN (residual) |
| — | **AMS contact setters under raw amsart derivatives (next batch; 86c r1, reverted)**: a raw class read over the AMS binding re-dresses `\address`, `\curraddr`, `\email`, `\urladdr` as stores only its own `\@maketitle` prints (stacs.cls:294-298, stacs_proc.cls, rmmcart.cls; m2an.cls, edpsmath.cls, pcms-l.cls:481 `\csname @address\number\addresscount@`), and our deposit of that `\@maketitle` is dropped, so the addresses are printed nowhere (0902.1737, 0802.2833). A lock on the four against the files (`"{cs}:locked@files"`) keeps them, but alone it imposes the binding's argument meaning on classes whose arguments differ — reverted until it ships with: (1) a stacs/stacs_proc binding: `\address[ref]{…}` an address with no name (the key is matched against `\author[ref]`, never printed; the lock made it a visible `<contact name="uniroma">`, 0902.1737's `lab1`–`lab4` in 0802.2833), and `\author[ref]{short}{long}` (stacs.cls:256) the long name as the person (today a stray long-name paragraph); (2) a pcms-l `\auth` binding (pcms-l.cls:203, reached through the paper's `\renewcommand{\auth}{\secdef\@auth\@sauth}`, pcmslmod-modified.tex:89), else the address goes to a nameless creator ahead of all (math0505518, math0602226); (3) OD #803's empty-shell drop extended to the deposit's footnotes and pubnotes whose content became contacts (m2an_mod.cls:118 `\footnote{\box\@b@ddrss@}`: an empty `<note role="footnote" mark="2">` in 2502.17950, 2309.08130, 2411.09498, math0701227; an empty `<pubnote/>` in 2305.18054, 2604.25825); (4) the lock with a wrapper opt-in that lets a file's redefinition through when it still calls the binding through a `\let`-saved copy (epigamath.sty:227-232, 2206.02674, 2304.03539, 2305.12241; MyCommA.sty:530-533, 2602.16384), where S1: the call must be real — at brace depth 0 and outside a conditional, not a mention (`sectioning-frontmatter/{ams_raw_class_address_setter_conditional, ams_raw_class_address_setter_deferred_call}`, review probes `p_cond`, `p_wrapstore`); S2: a wrapper installed with `\let\address\wr@address` takes the same test on `let_i`'s `:locked@files` path (`ams_package_let_wrapper_keeps_both`, `p_letwrap`); S3: a class's `\AtBeginDocument{\renewcommand\address…}` runs at the document's origin (OD #596) and bypasses the lock — fix or record (`ams_raw_class_address_atbegindocument`, `p_atbegin`). Boundaries the lock must keep: the document's own redefinition (1212.1375, 2206.01924; `ams_document_address_redefinition_stays`), a package's wrapper (`ams_package_wrapping_setters_keeps_both`). Repros (no guard): `sectioning-frontmatter/ams_raw_class_address_setters_stored` (RED) and the six above; the r1 code is branch `pk/86c-r1-backup` (state.rs `calls_locked_meaning`, ams_support_sty.rs). Its census (`ab_66bundle/pop86c.tsv`, 880 AMS-derived/redefining papers + a 292-paper amsart sample): 14 changed, also 1909.04033, 2001.07042, 2609.01974 and 2411.00367 (d-omega3.sty: E 18 → 9, `\organization`/`\orcid` still lost). Review `agent_reports/2026-10-28_review_86c_r1.md` (B1–B3, S1–S3); from the r2 re-review: B1's visible `name` label is what Perl itself gives 0902.1737 (it never reads stacs.cls, 1 missing file, and keeps `\address[ref]`'s key as the contact name), so the stacs binding goes beyond Perl rather than restoring parity; its guard must assert `@name` (the `creators()` helper cannot see it); the lock paths that held in r1 (`\let\address\relax` + `\newcommand`, a `\renewcommand` in a class option; probes `p_relaxnew`, `p_option`) are its boundaries; 2305.18054's thanks note loses its "∗" mark and its two-name `\author`'s address attaches to the last name only | open |

The spelling helper is shared by today's output and the FM S2 shadow. Fix: fold the German umlaut transliterations (ä→ae, ö→oe, ü→ue, ß→ss) into the comparison when the plain fold fails. FM S2a.2 r6 decision 3 sample | open |
| — | Graphics search order: LaTeX's `\IfFileExists` tries the bare name in the working directory first (kpathsea, latex.ltx:1095-1102) and only then the `\input@path`/`\graphicspath` entries (`\@iffileonpath`, latex.ltx:9698), where `image_candidates` (`util/image.rs:36-41`, Perl Image.pm:46-48 alike) lists `\graphicspath` entries first and SOURCEDIRECTORY last: with `\graphicspath{{../up/}{./}}` and a `u.pdf` in both, pdflatex reads `./u.pdf`, ours `../up/u.pdf` (for the extensionless name and the `.eps`-retry alike). Older than 78v (int63a the same). Also `graphics_type_rank` ranks `.MPS` among the uppercase types, which pdftex.def:599 does not list (no practical effect). Review rev78v3 findings 2 and 4 (probe `rev78v3/g/sub/g3.tex`) | open |
| — | float.sty's `\restylefloat{<type>}` of a kernel float (figure, table) records the style only (float_sty.rs `record_float_style`), so without caption the caption keeps the format it had: a ruled figure "Fig. 1: text" under IEEEtran (kernel ": " under article) where float.sty's `\floatc@ruled` prints "Fig. 1 text" (review rev78y probe3/rs.tex); the plain style's ": " happens to match. float.sty's own `\newfloat` types get the style's format (float_sty.rs `\lx@float@newfloat`, Perl float.sty.ltxml:50-53, whose plain format puts a space before the text and whose `boxed` style reads " " where float.sty prints ": "). Fix: give an unstarred `\restylefloat` the `\newfloat` type's style format. Also: a `\restylefloat` in the document body is not seen, since the class format is installed at `\begin{document}` keeping only the types restyled by then (caption_sty.rs `class_makecaption`, `\lx@class@floatsty@kept`): under IEEEtran `\restylefloat{figure}` before a second figure leaves "Fig. 2. " where pdflatex prints "Fig. 2: ", and `\restylefloat*{table}` after a `\restylefloat{table}` leaves "TABLE II: " where pdflatex prints "TABLE II" over the text (78z probe `scratch_78z/rs/rs.tex`; the preamble forms are right, guard `perfect_kernel_batch78y::ieeetran_restylefloat_table_keeps_float_sty_format`). 78y r2 | OPEN (pre-existing; found at 78y r2) |
| — | acmart copies older than TL 2025's v2.16 (v1.55-era, review rev78y acmcls/cs12.cls) set sigchi's small caption fonts on figures only, not tables; acmart_cls.rs ports the TL version's. Fonts only, so behind the class caption fonts knob (caption_sty.rs `CLASS_CAPTION_FONTS`, off by default): no output difference unless the knob is set. 78y r2 | OPEN (knob-only; per-version port not planned) |
| — | A preload's options do not reach a package another preload already loaded, in either order, so until 78z the class caption fonts knob could not be combined with ar5iv.sty: `--preload=[classcaptionfonts]latexml.sty --preload=ar5iv.sty` sets the knob but loses every option ar5iv.sty passes to latexml.sty (`ids`, `rawstyles`, `rawclasses`, `magnify`/`zoomout`, the limits — `\documentclass{paper}` falls to OmniBus with `Warning:missing_file` where ar5iv alone reads paper.cls raw; the magnify PIs and `<p>`/autoref ids gone), and `--preload=ar5iv.sty --preload=[classcaptionfonts]latexml.sty` sets no knob (no `\ds@classcaptionfonts` runs). Mechanism: `core_interface.rs:1085-1092` puts a preload's options into `opt@<name>.<ext>` only, `InputDefinitionOptions.options` stays empty, so binding/content.rs `apply_repeat_options` returns at its first test (`content.rs:602`); the repeat load is not re-read. Perl alike in effect (Core.pm:333-335 passes `options =>`; a repeat load only reports the "Option clash" Info, Package.pm:2586-2590, and is not re-read, Package.pm:2357). Relaxing every declared `\ds@` handler after `\ProcessOptions` is Perl's (Package.pm:2479-2481, ported at `content.rs:3339-3342`; latex.ltx:18566-18568 empties only the used ones) and not the cause. Fix for the knob (78z): ar5iv.sty declares `classcaptionfonts`/`noclasscaptionfonts` and passes the one given to latexml.sty with its own options (`ar5iv_sty.rs` `pass_options`), so `--preload=[classcaptionfonts]ar5iv.sty` sets the knob and keeps ar5iv's options (OD #884). Pre-existing; reachable since 78y r2 (review rev78y2 finding 1, probes `k1_*`, `k2_*`) | the class caption fonts knob FIXED 78z (guards `perfect_kernel_batch78z::{ar5iv_classcaptionfonts_option_sets_the_knob, ar5iv_classcaptionfonts_option_keeps_ar5iv_options}`); the general repeat-preload behaviour OPEN |
| — | Determinism (det354 B2): the `xmlns:*` declarations on the root `<document>` are in the order of the model's namespace table (`common/model.rs:1244-1254`, a `SymHashMap` at `:55`, → `document.rs:774-830`), not the order the document first uses them, as Perl declares them (Document.pm:1393-1394,1874): `\usepackage{fontawesome,tikz}` then `\faTimes … \tikz\draw (0,0)--(1,1);` declares `xmlns:svg` before `xmlns:aria`, though `aria` is used first (trigger det354 `repro/ns.tex`; Perl declares `svg` only). Stable today across int32a–int64a, since the symbols are created at init — a latent binary-to-binary instability, and golden churn on every SVG+aria document when changed. Fix: a document-order walk, with the prefixes of streaming-spilled content sorted after it; or sort by prefix (simpler, not Perl's). Not in 78w (main-session decision 2026-10-22) | open |
| — | appendix.sty: `\appendix` inside `{appendices}` does not restart the letters (pdflatex "A" again for the next section, ours "B": the kernel's `\@appendix` is `\relax` there; 2112.09636); pinned as ours by `perfect_kernel_batch79d::cleveref_appendix_inside_appendices_names_appendix` (review rev79d2 finding 8) | OPEN (pre-existing) |
| — | cleveref's `\appendix` type under TeX Live's LaTeX first aid: TL 2025's first aid (`\firstaid@cref@updatelabeldata`) drops cleveref's appendix aliasing, so TL 2025 pdflatex prints "section" after `\appendix` where first-aid-off pdflatex prints "appendix" (the oracle: the witnesses refer by `\ref`, so their PDFs never print the type; 2503.13751, pdfTeX-1.40.25 / TL 2023, only dates the kernel); 79d follows the latter (main-session decision 2). Decide by the build's TeX Live year if arXiv's builds move to a first-aid kernel (79d r2 writer's question 2) | open, low |
| — | Two adjacent `\vec`s (and `\mathbf`s) merge into one bold identifier by the kernel's `\mathbf` merge rule: 0812.2480 `e^{-i\vec{k}\vec{r}}` → one `<XMTok font="bold">kr</XMTok>`, 2305.06325 `\vec{g}\vec{z}` → "gz", `\vec{z}\vec{p}` → "zp"; `$\mathbf{k}\mathbf{r}$` gives "kr" in base (probe `rev79b/kr/`). Unlike the accepted `\vec{kr}` ruling, two vectors written separately lose their implied product; renders the same, a parser-semantics question (review rev79b (`agent_reports/extracted_356a0c47/2026-10-23T024112_a8b3383dff3c35201.md`, probes `~/.claude/jobs/356a0c47/tmp/rev79b/`), finding 3) | OPEN, needs a ruling; math parsing parked (no change) |
| — | euler.sty:47-57 and fourier.sty:77-87/104-114 declare the capital Greek letters `\mathord` (class 0) from their upright fonts, so in TeX `\mathbf{\Gamma}` keeps the medium-weight Euler/`futm` letter (and `\mathit{\Gamma}` stays upright; mathptmx's, mathpazo's and eulervm's `\mathord` `\up…` the same); ours follows the kernel's `\mathalpha` rule (`declare_capital_greek(…, None)`: `\mathbf` bold, `\mathit` italic), as Perl and int75a do — no regression, no fixed-upright `LettersClass` (review rev79e finding 3, probes `~/.claude/jobs/356a0c47/tmp/rev79e/`) | open, low (not a regression) |
| — | mathastext's Greek options (`symbolgreek`, `eulergreek`, `LGRgreek`, `selfGreek`, `itGreek`) are not modelled — its binding is a no-op for the capital Greek letters (79e r2 report); no census paper combines mathastext with the italic classes; a `\mathord` upright letter (mathptmx's `\up…`, amstex, euler) under `\mathit` turns italic here where TeX keeps it upright (no fixed-upright rule; the row above's gap) | open, low |
| — | The kernel's `\DeclareMathSymbol` gives a `\mathalpha` (class 7) symbol role `ID`, where Perl's (latex_constructs.pool.ltxml:2697-2705) gives none: a raw class/package copy's capital Greek letters (and any `\mathalpha` glyph) parse as `ID`, a binding's (`declare_capital_greek`, the kernel's `\Gamma`) as `UNKNOWN`. Predates 79e (55h); a parser role, math parsing parked (79e r1 writer's question 2, review rev79e decision 2: leave it, track here) | open, math parked |
| 25, 26, 27 | `--splitindex`/`--splitbibliography` (non-default), alignment open at `\end{document}`, lstlisting in `\parbox` | not silent, re-checked on head 2026-10-19: #26 is an honest Error (a `tabular` left open, `c & d`: 4 Errors; an `align`, `c&=d`: 3 — "Attempt to close a group that switched to mode internal_vertical"; invalid input, pdflatex errs); #27 has raised 1 Error since 64zf (CONTROL `kernel-alignment/pcol_lstlisting_parbox_CONTROL`, pdflatex 7); #25 needs a non-default option the arXiv runs never pass — open, low reach, not blocking |

## Rust Error Fixes

Rust-only defects (a port that diverged from the Perl it translates, not a Perl-origin defect), each with its
KNOWN_PERL_ERRORS cross-reference when one applies. Perl-origin defects go to `parity/KNOWN_PERL_ERRORS.md`.

- **An `\xymatrix` cell opening with `\xy` lost the wrapper's conditional (82f)**: xy_sty.rs's `\xy` wrapper
  (`\if\inxy@\lx@xy@svgnested\else\lx@xy@svg\fi\lx@xy@original`, LaTeXML's own) at a cell head was expanded by the
  alignment's peek and its conditional was lost before the `\else` in xymatrix's two-pass `\halign` — 10 errors for
  `$\xymatrix{\xy *{a} \endxy}$`, conditional-limit Fatals in 1109.4916 and 1611.07444 (a raw `\halign` keeps such a
  conditional: probed). The wrapper opens with `\relax`, as `\endxy`'s does. Guard
  `perfect_kernel_batch81::an_xymatrix_cell_may_open_with_xy`.
- **A display's `$$` deferred past an author's open group (82g)**: tex.web §1193 + §1064-1065 `off_save` closes a group
  opened in the formula at its math shift; done for inline formulas (OXIDIZED_DESIGN #553), a display's end waited for
  the group's own end, which a typo (`$$ H^k{X,\C/\Z) $$`, 1309.7562) never reaches — 101 errors and a Fatal, pdflatex 1.
  A `$$` display outside alignments carries `DISPLAY_FORMULA` and takes the same path (`formula_reachable`). Guard
  `perfect_kernel_batch81::a_display_math_shift_closes_a_group_opened_in_it`.
- **`\newcommand{\cs[n]}{…}` (82h)**: KNOWN_PERL_ERRORS #1192; `LaTeXDefToken` adds latex.ltx's `\@testopt{…}0` step
  (1312.5793: 90 errors and a Fatal → 0).
- **`\pdftexbanner` empty (82i)**: Perl's is too (pdfTeX.pool.ltxml:93); it now names the runtime tree's TeX Live year
  and that release's versions, which 2309.17381 parses for the year (an error-limit Fatal before). Guard
  `perfect_kernel_batch81::pdftexbanner_names_the_texlive_year`.
- **A package by its installed path missed its binding (82j)**: `\usepackage{graphics/graphicx}` raw-loaded graphicx.sty
  over our graphics binding and the first `\rotatebox` exhausted memory (2309.12794); `source_tree_basename` takes the
  basename when the path resolves to the same file and the basename has a binding. Guard
  `perfect_kernel_batch81::a_package_by_its_installed_path_uses_its_binding`.
- **An xargs optional-argument command expanded outside typesetting (82c)**: xargs.sty:205-212 defines a
  `\newcommandx` command with any optional argument as `\@protected@testopt\cs…`/`\@protected@testopt@xargs\cs…`
  (:20-25), unexpanded whenever `\protect` is not `\@typeset@protect`; our binding built a plain macro and the gullet's
  `optional_arg_protected` saw only a leading `Optional` parameter, so `\sequence[2][2 = undefined]` (plain first
  argument) expanded inside ifthen's `\xdef` of the outer `\set`'s test and ran its own `\ifthenelse`: Fatal "File ended
  while scanning use of a Until:\NOT argument" (2002.09653). The first parameter is flagged `testopt` when any is
  optional and the gullet honours the flag. Guard `perfect_kernel_batch81::an_xargs_optional_command_is_protected_in_an_ifthen_test`.
- **A LaTeX picture inside PiCTeX kept PiCTeX's `\put` (82d)**: postpictex.tex:39-46 redefines `\@picture` to give a
  LaTeX picture LaTeX's `\put`, `\multiput`, `\frame` and `\linethickness` back; our `{picture}` (sect13.rs) bypasses
  `\@picture`, so 1301.4518's `\put{$\begin{picture}(50,50)\put(3,0){…}\end{picture}$} at 3.5 -1` read the inner `\put`
  as PiCTeX's, which scanned to the input's end for `at` (a pushback-limit Fatal; pdflatex 0). The environment makes
  those `\let`s when PiCTeX saved them (`\!latex@picture`). Guard
  `perfect_kernel_batch81::a_latex_picture_inside_pictex_keeps_latex_put`.
- **hyperref's autoref tag ran the document's `\null` (81b)**: `\lx@autorefnum@@` (hyperref_sty.rs), the autoref text
  every numbered target builds (OXIDIZED_DESIGN_DIVERGENCES #418), ends with the token `\null` (the delimiter of the
  `\def\equationautorefname~#1\null{…}` idiom, DIVERGENCES #98); Perl's formatter ends with none. pdflatex runs `\null`
  only where an `\autoref` prints, so 1501.00090's `\def\null0{ \subsection{Other formats} … }` (hiding a subsection)
  ran its `\subsection` in every `\section`'s tag: Fatal Stomach:Recursion, no creators kept. The tag saves the
  document's `\null`, runs plain.tex's `\hbox{}` copy `\lx@autoref@null` and restores it by `\let` (no group: a name
  opening a `\bgroup` would meet it, 78j). Guard `perfect_kernel_batch81::an_authors_null_stays_out_of_the_autoref_tag`
  (repro cross-references/hyperref_autoref_tag_runs_the_kernel_null). Seen: that `\hbox{}` leaves an empty
  `<text xml:id="S1.1"/>` in every hyperref document's autoref tags (OPEN_ROWS frontmatter-adjacent singleton (h)).
- **A creator's fuzzy name label leaked a mark's comma (80v)**: `Tag ltx:creator after_close` (author_macros.rs) appends
  `fuzzy:<name text>` to the comma list `_annotations`, as Perl does (Base_Utility.pool.ltxml:873); a name showing
  `$^{a,b}$` left the bare label `b`, which our by-mark respelling in `relocate_annotations` (Rust-only) read as that
  name's own claim on the `$^b$` line, so a second name marked `b` lost it (0806.3762: Pujolas). The label is one label
  now (its commas `_`). Guard `perfect_kernel_batch80a::revtex_marked_address_goes_by_mark`. The leak had masked three
  by-mark gaps, fixed with it (80y): a marked line's key was read from its tokens joined without the boundary after a
  control word (`$^{\rm a}$` → `\rma`, no key: `\lx@sup@setlabel@affiliation` reads the TeX spelling now, 2008.00493);
  spacing in or before a name's script was no mark (`{}^{a,\;b}`, `{}^{\rm b,\ c}`, `\,{}^{b,c}`: read from the XMath since 90a, #911,
  1307.6166, hep-th0410034, 2311.14432); a bracketed number was none (`$^{[1]}$` against `$^1$`, 1803.06822). Guard
  `perfect_kernel_batch80a::marked_list_marks_link_however_spelt`.
- **The FM writer left a lone suffixed id (80u)**: an item today's frontmatter places under several creators is numbered
  once per copy (`footnotex3`, `modify_id`'s `footnotex3a`); the writer taking the copy that held the plain id left the
  other's suffix standing alone (1606.04500, 1607.07280). `writer::apply::reclaim_ids` gives the taken ids back to the
  copy that stays. Guard `perfect_kernel_batchfms2b::footnote_marked_affiliations_go_to_their_names`.

- **cleveref `\crefname`/`\Crefname` derivation (77s)**: our clean port (`cref_define_name`, cleveref_sty.rs) dropped
  cleveref.sty:1296-1325's derivation of the other variant (Perl loads the raw macro), so a lone
  `\crefname{appendixtable}{Appendix Table}{…}` left `\Cref` with no name (2404.11739). Derived since 77s, changing case
  for both names or neither as the singular is empty or not (cleveref.sty:1309). Related Perl defect: KPE #998 (the
  `\label[type]` it names). Guards `perfect_kernel_batch77s::{cleveref_label_type_names_the_reference,
  cleveref_derived_name_follows_the_singular}`.
- **An unnumbered heading's title font (78a)**: our `\@@unnumbered@section` (sect04.rs) dropped Perl's
  `\lx@format@title@font@@` (latex_constructs.pool.ltxml:666-670), so a starred section lost its class font (IEEEtran's
  small caps, sectsty's and titlesec's formats; ≈61,800 IEEEtran papers with a `\section*`). Restored, with titlesec's
  after-code (OD #785) and the title braced as a numbered one's. Guards `perfect_kernel_batch78a::{
  unnumbered_section_takes_the_title_font, titlesec_after_code_closes_an_unnumbered_title}`.
- **Text merged into a text node is ligated (78a)**: where libxml merged an insertion into the last text child
  (`open_text_internal`), the parent stayed current, so the run was never closed and never ligated — text after a
  formula `cleanup_math` turns into spaces (`500$\,$hPa 1989--2008`, nucl-ex0511052, 2208.08472). The merged node is
  current now, its run ligated from where it starts (Perl `setNode`, Document.pm openText_internal). Guard
  `perfect_kernel_batch78a::text_after_a_space_only_formula_is_ligatured`.
- **`\halign` inserts `\everycr`; AMS-TeX's active `@` (78b)**: the `\halign` primitive inserts `\everycr` after its

- **subfig's `\newsubfloat` was a no-op (78s)**: our subfig_sty.rs stubbed it (Perl subfig.sty.ltxml:30-62 makes the

- **A class copy with classic Mac line ends was one comment (79b)**: `shipped_class::class_code` split lines at line

- **An argument a template's cell brace closes (79y)**: 69c carries `{eqnarray}`'s and amsmath's cell braces

  `&` ends the cell mid-argument (tex.web §342) and the v-part's `}` must end the argument. gullet.rs

  (`hluatex` under the `luatex` profile), `hxetex` under XeTeX, else the last DVI driver option, the class's global ones

  SHIP (driver matched latex/pdflatex/xelatex/lualatex on every probe; PDF-persona probes byte-identical to base). Also

- **A recovered Fatal left the Stomach's token stack full (int131fix)**: `invoke_token` (stomach.rs) pops its

## Open items — detail for the ranked rows

### R3 — Bibliography-Absence Campaign (PR #444 Residuals)
- **R3d — RE-MEASURED + CLOSED int26 (agent_reports/2026-10-18_r3d_remeasure.md):** of the 28 papers
  (`docs/parity/bib_absence_2026-07-29/residual_characterization.tsv.gz`, `\lx@begin@alignment` rows) 27 convert to the
  end on head with their bibliographies; the last, 2606.03447, was physics' unguarded `[…]` read (KNOWN_PERL_ERRORS
  #981), fixed in int26 (`phys_read_optional`). The mechanism below was wrong: TeX's `macro_call` does not disable tab
  marks (that is `read_toks`, tex.web §482); ltcmd's `\group_align_safe_begin:` (latex.ltx:2103-2110) does, so a global
  guard in `Parameters::read_arguments` would be unfaithful (settled dead end; the 5 regressions were correct).
- **(historical) R3d: Alignment Parameter Scan vs Cell Read Distinction (`suppressed_tab_marks`):**
  - *Symptom:* An unescaped `&` inside a delimiter-fenced macro argument splits the alignment row and truncates the document (and bibliography). 12 papers remain affected.
  - *Mechanism:* `tex.web` §394 `macro_call` suppresses tab marks while scanning parameters. `SuppressedTabMarks` in [`latexml_core/src/common/local_assignments.rs:194`](../latexml_core/src/common/local_assignments.rs#L194) fixed `physics.sty`'s `\mqty` (14 papers), but applying it globally to `Parameters::read_arguments` regressed 5 tests (`cells_test`, `numprints_test`, `xytest_test`, `consort_flowchart_test`, `unit_tests_by_silviu_test`) because that path also reads alignment cell content.
  - *Action:* Distinguish macro parameter scanning from alignment cell reading in `Parameters::read_arguments` so tab marks inside `{...}` do not split outer cells.
- **R3b — RE-MEASURED + CLOSED 2026-10-18 (agent_reports/2026-10-18_r3b_rootcause.md):** the six "real `\cite`, empty
  bibliography" papers all keep their bibliographies on 67int27 — 2605.14990 53, 2606.05629 19, 2606.10056 26,
  2606.17491 30, 2606.00231 31 bibitems, each = pdflatex+bibtex's `.bbl`, 0 E/F (count them in the post-processed
  output: the core XML's `<bibliography files=…>` is a placeholder latexml_post fills from the `.bib`). 2605.29754
  comments out biblatex's `\printbibliography` and types its list by hand (kept); its `\cite`s render the keys with
  `Warning:expected:ids Missing Entry`, as Perl (SHARED, honest; biblatex labels without an `ltx:bibliography` would be
  a 1-paper surpass, not pursued). Side finding, open: tlp.cls `\maketitle` leaves a group open — a Rust-only
  `Warning:unexpected:\end{document} … inside a group at level 1` (2606.10056; content intact; suspect the locked
  `\maketitle` replaying tlp.cls:519-536 `\@ifnextchar [{\m@ketitleone}…` through `\lx@deposit@maketitle`; repro
  scratch_356a0c47/r3b/repro_tlp/a.tex).
- **R3g: amsrefs bare `\begin{biblist}` — FIXED (71a):** a `{biblist}` outside `{bibdiv}` opens its own bibliography
  through the backmatter route (amsrefs_sty.rs; KNOWN_PERL_ERRORS #939).

### R4 — `--preload=<cls>` trips the LaTeX hook stack (`Extra \PopDefaultHookLabel`)
- **Symptom:** `--preload=article.cls` prints `LaTeX hooks Error: Extra \PopDefaultHookLabel` (clean without preload or with `LATEXML_NODUMP=1`).
- **Mechanism:** `\@pushfilename` changes meaning mid-load: `article` is pushed before `LaTeX.pool` loads, using a pre-pool `\@pushfilename` that does not touch `\g__hook_name_stack_seq`. The pool installs expl3's `\@popfilename`, which pops an empty seq and errors.
- **Resolution:** A TeX-side repair or re-synchronizing the sequence at the point `LoadPool('LaTeX')` executes.

### R6 — `Collector::rescan` Refactor for Generated Backmatter
- **Symptom:** Generated subtrees (Bibliographies, Indexes, Glossaries) lose ObjectDB relations, labels, and fragids because `Scan` owns `ObjectDB` by value (`latexml_post/src/scan.rs:49`) and cannot rescan generated nodes.
- **Resolution:** Refactor `Scan` to borrow or take/restore `ObjectDB`, enabling clean rescanning of generated nodes and removing fragile ad-hoc registrations.

### R7 — Presentation-MathML F5 Linebreaker
- **Status:** The only remaining pMML gap from the MathML line audit (F17 is closed). A full linebreaker algorithm gap requiring a port-or-drop scope decision before coding.
